> AGENT

The doc reads cleanly. Here's a summary of the three simplifications I made:

**1. Pipeline owns its own logging.** `append_log_entry` is engine-layer code, so the pipeline can call it directly. This eliminates `_log_pipeline_changes` from the scan caller and `_append_workflow_log_entries` from the import caller. Each caller only writes its own unique log entries.

**2. Killed `_PlanImportMutations` and the adapter layer.** The old plan kept `_PlanImportMutations`, added `_merge_pipeline_into_mutations` to copy fields in, then had log/display functions read from both. Now the import flow just holds two values — `import_result` and `pipeline_result` — and passes them directly to display and logging functions. No intermediate struct, no field copying, no `_record_workflow_change`.

**3. Straightened the import flow to a linear pipeline.** Steps are now: load → review delta → pipeline → save → log → display. Two result objects flow straight through with no merging. The function body should read almost like pseudocode.

The net effect: the pipeline is ~50 lines of clean orchestration that owns both gate execution and logging. The scan caller drops from 6 `_sync_*_and_log` wrappers to 1 display function. The import caller drops from 7 helpers (`_PlanImportMutations`, `_record_workflow_change`, `_merge_pipeline_into_mutations`, `_sync_subjective_queue_after_import`, `_sync_lifecycle_phase_after_import`, `_has_postflight_review_work`, `_append_workflow_log_entries`) to 0 new helpers — just updated signatures on existing display/log functions.

> DEVELOPER

is this a beautiful solution?

> AGENT

No. It's good engineering — correct, well-documented, simpler than where we started. But it's not beautiful. Here's why.

**The skip flags are a tell.** `skip_subjective`, `skip_auto_cluster` mean the abstraction doesn't quite fit. The two callers want different subsets of the pipeline, and we're papering over that with booleans. Beautiful abstractions don't need "please skip step 2" flags.

**`PostflightContext` is lopsided.** It has 5 fields. The scan path uses 1 (`current_scores`). The import path uses all 5. This is an import-specific type wearing a shared-pipeline costume.

**The pipeline is a shallow wrapper, not a deep abstraction.** It calls 7 engine functions in sequence, writes log entries, and returns a kitchen-sink result dataclass. The callers still need to know which fields to check, how to interpret `triage.deferred` vs `triage.injected`, and how to thread results into their display functions. The mental model isn't simpler — it's just relocated.

**The real duplication is thinner than it looks.** Scan calls 6 engine functions in sequence. Import calls 6 engine functions in sequence. That's not structural duplication — that's two callers using the same API. That's what APIs are for.

The plan is in an uncanny valley: substantial enough to require new types, a new module, behavioral delta analysis, and 15+ test cases — but not deep enough to actually simplify how you think about the system.

---

**What would be beautiful?**

Option A: **Just fix the bug.** The actual problem is that the import path injects triage before `create-plan`. The fix is:

1. Add `inject_triage: bool = True` to `sync_plan_after_review_import`
2. In `sync_plan_after_import`, pass `inject_triage=False` and call `sync_triage_needed` after `sync_create_plan_needed`

That's it. Five lines. The "duplication" between scan and import is coincidental — they call the same engine functions but for different reasons, with different conditions. Coincidental duplication doesn't need unification.

Option B: **Go deeper.** If the duplication really bothers us, the beautiful version would make the pipeline so natural that no skip flags exist. Something like: each gate decides for itself whether to fire based on the plan/state it receives. The pipeline is just "run all gates" — no caller-specific configuration. The gates are already mostly like this (they check their own preconditions internally). The problem is that `auto_cluster_issues` doesn't have its own mid-cycle guard — the caller provides it. If we pushed that guard into the gate itself, the pipeline would be:

```python
def run_postflight_gates(plan, state, *, policy, target_strict, cycle_just_completed=False):
    subjective = sync_subjective_dimensions(plan, state, policy=policy, cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed)
    auto_clusters = auto_cluster_issues(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict, policy=policy)
    communicate = sync_communicate_score_needed(plan, state, policy=policy, ...)
    create_plan = sync_create_plan_needed(plan, state, policy=policy)
    triage = sync_triage_needed(plan, state, policy=policy)
    phase = coarse_phase_name(queue_context(state, plan=plan, target_strict=target_strict).snapshot.phase)
    set_lifecycle_phase(plan, phase)
    ...
```

No skip flags, no context object. Every gate is self-gating. The pipeline is a plain sequence. Callers call it and read the result. That's beautiful — but it requires pushing mid-cycle/data-change guards into the engine functions themselves, which is a different (and possibly better) refactor.

---

My honest recommendation: **Option A for now.** Fix the bug, accept the coincidental duplication. The plan as written would leave the codebase *more correct* but not *more elegant*. If the duplication starts causing real maintenance pain later, do Option B — push guards into the gates and make the pipeline truly parameterless.

Want me to rewrite the task doc as a minimal bug fix, or push toward Option B?

> DEVELOPER

that's not beatuiful

> AGENT

You're right. Let me think about this differently.

The pipeline function in the plan takes 8 parameters, including a 5-field context object and 2 skip flags. That's not a unification — it's a function that knows about both callers and switches behavior based on which one is calling. The callers are still coupled to the pipeline's internals.

The beautiful version comes from one insight: **the pipeline shouldn't need to know what triggered it.**

Everything the gates need is already in the plan and state — or could be. The import caller already writes `pending_import_scores` to `refresh_state` for `sync_import_scores_needed`. That pattern generalizes. Instead of passing `scores_just_imported=True` as a parameter, the import pre-step writes `refresh_state.rebaseline_scores = {...}`. The gate reads it, acts on it, clears it. `cycle_just_completed` is just `not plan.get("plan_start_scores")`. `mid_cycle` is `is_mid_cycle(plan)`. `current_scores` is `score_snapshot(state)`. All derivable.

So the pipeline is:

```python
def reconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict):
```

Three arguments. No context object. No skip flags. It computes everything it needs from what's in front of it. The gates fire or don't based on plan/state, not caller identity.

And the callers become:

```python
# scan
reset_if_force_rescan(plan)
reconcile_issues(plan, state)
result = reconcile_plan(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict)
display_scan_results(result, plan)
sync_plan_start_scores(plan, state)
sync_postflight_completion(plan, state)
save(plan)

# import
import_result = sync_review_delta(plan, state)
write_import_context(plan, trusted=..., assessment_mode=...)
result = reconcile_plan(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict)
display_import_results(import_result, result)
save(plan)
```

Each caller is 5-6 lines. Pre-step → reconcile → post-step → save → display. The reconciliation doesn't know or care what happened before it. It just makes the plan consistent with reality.

Should I rewrite the doc around this?

> DEVELOPER

But does it not need to know if it's pre or post flight - so it knwos ot show the score? will it only show scan or subjective review items only in this pre or post flight one

> AGENT

Good challenge. But no — the gates already know this intrinsically.

`sync_communicate_score_needed` checks `policy.unscored_ids`. Preflight, dimensions are unscored, so the gate doesn't fire. Postflight, reviews are done, so it may fire. The gate's own preconditions handle it — nobody needs to tell it "this is postflight."

Same for every other gate:
- `sync_subjective_dimensions`: `cycle_just_completed = not plan.get("plan_start_scores")` — derivable from plan. Preflight it injects unscored dimensions, postflight it injects stale/under-target. Same function, different behavior based on state.
- `auto_cluster_issues`: `is_mid_cycle(plan)` — derivable from plan. No-op mid-cycle.
- `sync_create_plan_needed`: checks `policy.unscored_ids` and `policy.has_objective_backlog` — won't fire until reviews are done and backlog exists.
- `sync_triage_needed`: checks issue changes since last triage baseline — self-gating.

The phase isn't an input to reconciliation. It's an **output**. The snapshot computes it from what's in the plan and state. Preflight/postflight is an emergent property, not a parameter.

That's what makes the three-argument version real and not forced — `reconcile_plan(plan, state, target_strict)` genuinely doesn't need to be told what phase it's in. It figures it out and does the right thing. The callers just mutate plan/state (scan adds issues, import adds reviews and writes context flags to `refresh_state`), then call reconcile. The reconciliation is always the same process — "make the plan consistent with reality."

The one thing that does need to change: `sync_communicate_score_needed` currently takes `scores_just_imported` and `current_scores` as parameters. The import pre-step would instead write `refresh_state.rebaseline_scores = {...}`, and the gate reads/consumes it. This is consistent with how `sync_import_scores_needed` already works — it reads `pending_import_scores` from `refresh_state`.

   100→    issues_only_audit: dict[str, Any] | None,
   101→) -> PendingImportScoresMeta:
   102→    packet_sha256 = ""
   103→    if isinstance(import_payload, dict):
   104→        raw_provenance = import_payload.get("provenance")
   105→        if isinstance(raw_provenance, dict):
   106→            packet_sha256 = str(raw_provenance.get("packet_sha256", "")).strip()
   107→    recorded_file = (
   108→        str(import_file).strip()
   109→        if isinstance(import_file, str) and import_file.strip()
   110→        else str(issues_only_audit.get("import_file", "")).strip()
   111→        if isinstance(issues_only_audit, dict)
   112→        else ""
   113→    )
   114→    timestamp = ""
   115→    if isinstance(issues_only_audit, dict):
   116→        timestamp = str(issues_only_audit.get("timestamp", "")).strip()
   117→    if not packet_sha256 and isinstance(issues_only_audit, dict):
   118→        packet_sha256 = str(issues_only_audit.get("packet_sha256", "")).strip()
   119→    return PendingImportScoresMeta(
   120→        timestamp=timestamp,
   121→        import_file=recorded_file,
   122→        normalized_import_file=_normalize_match_path(recorded_file) or "",
   123→        packet_sha256=packet_sha256,
   124→    )
   125→
   126→
   127→def pending_import_scores_meta(
   128→    plan: PlanModel,
   129→    state: StateModel,
   130→) -> PendingImportScoresMeta | None:
   131→    """Return queued score-import metadata without mutating the plan."""
   132→    refresh_state = _get_refresh_state(plan)
   133→    if refresh_state is not None:
   134→        meta = PendingImportScoresMeta.from_mapping(
   135→            refresh_state.get(_PENDING_IMPORT_SCORES_KEY)
   136→        )
   137→        if meta is not None:
   138→            return meta
   139→    issues_only_audit = _latest_assessment_audit(state, modes={"issues_only"})
   140→    if issues_only_audit is None:
   141→        return None
   142→    return _build_pending_import_scores_meta(
   143→        import_file=str(issues_only_audit.get("import_file", "")),
   144→        import_payload=None,
   145→        issues_only_audit=issues_only_audit,
   146→    )
   147→
   148→
   149→def import_scores_meta_matches(
   150→    meta: PendingImportScoresMeta | dict[str, Any] | None,
   151→    *,
   152→    import_file: str,
   153→    import_payload: dict[str, Any],
   154→) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   155→    """Return (matches, reason) for whether the import matches the pending batch.
   156→
   157→    Checks packet_sha256 first (strongest signal), falls back to normalized
   158→    file path.  Returns a single human-readable reason on mismatch.
   159→    """
   160→    normalized_meta = (
   161→        meta
   162→        if isinstance(meta, PendingImportScoresMeta)
   163→        else PendingImportScoresMeta.from_mapping(meta)
   164→    )
   165→    if normalized_meta is None:
   166→        return True, ""
   167→
   168→    provenance = import_payload.get("provenance")
   169→    provenance_dict = provenance if isinstance(provenance, dict) else {}
   170→
   171→    expected_hash = normalized_meta.packet_sha256
   172→    current_hash = str(provenance_dict.get("packet_sha256", "")).strip()
   173→    if expected_hash and current_hash:
   174→        if current_hash == expected_hash:
   175→            return True, ""
   176→        return False, f"expected packet_sha256 {expected_hash}, got {current_hash}"
   177→
   178→    expected_file = normalized_meta.normalized_import_file
   179→    current_file = _normalize_match_path(import_file) or ""
   180→    if expected_file and current_file and current_file != expected_file:
   181→        return False, f"expected import file {normalized_meta.import_file}, got {import_file}"
   182→
   183→    return True, ""
   184→
   185→
   186→def _clear_pending_import_scores(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   187→    order = plan["queue_order"]
   188→    if WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID in order:
   189→        order[:] = [item for item in order if item != WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID]
   190→    refresh_state = _get_refresh_state(plan)
   191→    if refresh_state is not None:
   192→        refresh_state.pop(_PENDING_IMPORT_SCORES_KEY, None)
   193→
   194→
   195→def _pending_compare_timestamp(
   196→    pending_meta: PendingImportScoresMeta | None,
   197→    latest_issues_only: dict[str, Any],
   198→) -> str:
   199→    if pending_meta is not None and pending_meta.timestamp:
   200→        return pending_meta.timestamp
   201→    return str(latest_issues_only.get("timestamp", "")).strip()
   202→
   203→
   204→def _pending_import_scores_stale(
   205→    *,
   206→    order: list[str],
   207→    pending_meta: PendingImportScoresMeta | None,
   208→    latest_issues_only: dict[str, Any] | None,
   209→    latest_trusted: dict[str, Any] | None,
   210→) -> bool:
   211→    if WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID not in order:
   212→        return False
   213→    if latest_issues_only is None:
   214→        return True
   215→    if latest_trusted is None:
   216→        return False
   217→
   218→    latest_trusted_ts = str(latest_trusted.get("timestamp", "")).strip()
   219→    compare_ts = _pending_compare_timestamp(pending_meta, latest_issues_only)
   220→    return bool(compare_ts and latest_trusted_ts and latest_trusted_ts >= compare_ts)
   221→
   222→
   223→def _record_pending_import_scores(
   224→    refresh_state: dict[str, Any],
   225→    *,
   226→    import_file: str | None,
   227→    import_payload: dict[str, Any] | None,
   228→    latest_issues_only: dict[str, Any] | None,
   229→) -> None:
   230→    refresh_state[_PENDING_IMPORT_SCORES_KEY] = _build_pending_import_scores_meta(
   231→        import_file=import_file,
   232→        import_payload=import_payload,
   233→        issues_only_audit=latest_issues_only,
   234→    ).to_dict()
   235→
   236→
   237→def _no_unscored(
   238→    state: StateModel,
   239→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None,
   240→) -> bool:
   241→    """Return True when no unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions remain."""
   242→    if policy is not None:
   243→        return not policy.unscored_ids
   244→    return not stale_policy_mod.current_unscored_ids(
   245→        state, subjective_prefix=SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX,
   246→    )
   247→
   248→
   249→def _inject(plan: PlanModel, item_id: str) -> QueueSyncResult:
   250→    """Inject *item_id* into the workflow prefix and clear stale skip entries."""
   251→    order = plan["queue_order"]
   252→    if item_id not in order:
   253→        order.append(item_id)
   254→    normalize_queue_workflow_and_triage_prefix(order)
   255→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   256→    if isinstance(skipped, dict):
   257→        skipped.pop(item_id, None)
   258→    return QueueSyncResult(injected=[item_id])
   259→
   260→
   261→_EMPTY = QueueSyncResult
   262→
   263→
   264→def sync_score_checkpoint_needed(
   265→    plan: PlanModel,
   266→    state: StateModel,
   267→    *,
   268→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   269→) -> QueueSyncResult:
   270→    """Inject ``workflow::score-checkpoint`` when all initial reviews complete.
   271→
   272→    Injects when:
   273→    - No unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions remain
   274→    - ``workflow::score-checkpoint`` is not already in the queue
   275→
   276→    Front-loads it into the workflow prefix so it stays ahead of triage.
   277→    """
   278→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   279→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   280→
   281→    if WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID in order:
   282→        return _EMPTY()
   283→    if not _no_unscored(state, policy):
   284→        return _EMPTY()
   285→    return _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_SCORE_CHECKPOINT_ID)
   286→
   287→
   288→def sync_create_plan_needed(
   289→    plan: PlanModel,
   290→    state: StateModel,
   291→    *,
   292→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   293→) -> QueueSyncResult:
   294→    """Inject ``workflow::create-plan`` when reviews complete + objective backlog exists.
   295→
   296→    Only injects when:
   297→    - No unscored (placeholder) subjective dimensions remain
   298→    - At least one objective issue exists
   299→    - ``workflow::create-plan`` is not already in the queue
   300→    - No triage stages are pending
   301→
   302→    Front-loads it into the workflow prefix so it stays ahead of triage.
   303→    """
   304→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   305→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   306→
   307→    if WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID in order:
   308→        return _EMPTY()
   309→    if any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS):
   310→        return _EMPTY()
   311→    if not _no_unscored(state, policy):
   312→        return _EMPTY()
   313→
   314→    if not has_objective_backlog(state, policy):
   315→        return _EMPTY()
   316→
   317→    return _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_CREATE_PLAN_ID)
   318→
   319→
   320→def sync_import_scores_needed(
   321→    plan: PlanModel,
   322→    state: StateModel,
   323→    *,
   324→    assessment_mode: str | None = None,
   325→    import_file: str | None = None,
   326→    import_payload: dict[str, Any] | None = None,
   327→) -> QueueSyncResult:
   328→    """Inject ``workflow::import-scores`` after issues-only import.
   329→
   330→    Only injects when:
   331→    - Assessment mode was ``issues_only`` (scores were skipped)
   332→    - ``workflow::import-scores`` is not already in the queue
   333→
   334→    Front-loads it into the workflow prefix so it stays ahead of triage.
   335→    """
   336→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   337→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   338→    refresh_state = _ensure_refresh_state(plan)
   339→    pending_meta = PendingImportScoresMeta.from_mapping(
   340→        refresh_state.get(_PENDING_IMPORT_SCORES_KEY)
   341→    )
   342→    latest_issues_only = _latest_assessment_audit(state, modes={"issues_only"})
   343→    latest_trusted = _latest_assessment_audit(state, modes=_TRUSTED_ASSESSMENT_MODES)
   344→
   345→    if _pending_import_scores_stale(
   346→        order=order,
   347→        pending_meta=pending_meta,
   348→        latest_issues_only=latest_issues_only,
   349→        latest_trusted=latest_trusted,
   350→    ):
   351→        _clear_pending_import_scores(plan)
   352→        return QueueSyncResult(pruned=[WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID])
   353→
   354→    if WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID in order:
   355→        if assessment_mode == "issues_only":
   356→            _record_pending_import_scores(
   357→                refresh_state,
   358→                import_file=import_file,
   359→                import_payload=import_payload,
   360→                latest_issues_only=latest_issues_only,
   361→            )
   362→            return QueueSyncResult(resurfaced=[WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID])
   363→        return _EMPTY()
   364→    if assessment_mode != "issues_only":
   365→        return _EMPTY()
   366→    result = _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_IMPORT_SCORES_ID)
   367→    _record_pending_import_scores(
   368→        refresh_state,
   369→        import_file=import_file,
   370→        import_payload=import_payload,
   371→        latest_issues_only=latest_issues_only,
   372→    )
   373→    return result
   374→
   375→
   376→class ScoreSnapshot:
   377→    """Minimal score snapshot for rebaseline — avoids importing state.py."""
   378→
   379→    __slots__ = ("strict", "overall", "objective", "verified")
   380→
   381→    def __init__(
   382→        self,
   383→        *,
   384→        strict: float | None,
   385→        overall: float | None,
   386→        objective: float | None,
   387→        verified: float | None,
   388→    ) -> None:
   389→        self.strict = strict
   390→        self.overall = overall
   391→        self.objective = objective
   392→        self.verified = verified
   393→
   394→
   395→def sync_communicate_score_needed(
   396→    plan: PlanModel,
   397→    state: StateModel,
   398→    *,
   399→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None,
   400→    scores_just_imported: bool = False,
   401→    current_scores: ScoreSnapshot | None = None,
   402→) -> QueueSyncResult:
   403→    """Inject ``workflow::communicate-score`` and rebaseline scores.
   404→
   405→    Injects when:
   406→    - All initial subjective reviews are complete (no unscored dims), OR
   407→      scores were just imported (trusted/attested/override)
   408→    - ``workflow::communicate-score`` is not already in the queue
   409→    - Score has not already been communicated this cycle
   410→      (``previous_plan_start_scores`` absent), unless a trusted score import
   411→      explicitly refreshed the live score mid-cycle
   412→
   413→    When injected and *current_scores* is provided, ``plan_start_scores``
   414→    is rebaselined to the current score so the score display unfreezes at
   415→    the new value.  The previous baseline is preserved in
   416→    ``previous_plan_start_scores`` so the communicate-score queue item can
   417→    show the old → new delta — and so mid-cycle scans know not to
   418→    re-inject.
   419→    """
   420→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   421→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   422→
   423→    if WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID in order:
   424→        return _EMPTY()
   425→    # Already communicated this cycle — previous_plan_start_scores is set
   426→    # at injection time and cleared at cycle boundaries.
   427→    if "previous_plan_start_scores" in plan and not scores_just_imported:
   428→        return _EMPTY()
   429→    if not scores_just_imported and not _no_unscored(state, policy):
   430→        return _EMPTY()
   431→
   432→    if current_scores is not None:
   433→        _rebaseline_plan_start_scores(plan, current_scores)
   434→    # Set sentinel even when rebaseline was a no-op (no plan_start_scores
   435→    # to rebaseline) so mid-cycle scans don't re-inject.
   436→    if not plan.get("previous_plan_start_scores"):
   437→        plan["previous_plan_start_scores"] = {}
   438→    return _inject(plan, WORKFLOW_COMMUNICATE_SCORE_ID)
   439→
   440→
   441→def _rebaseline_plan_start_scores(
   442→    plan: PlanModel,
   443→    scores: ScoreSnapshot,
   444→) -> None:
   445→    """Snapshot the current score as the new baseline, preserving the old one."""
   446→    old_start = plan.get("plan_start_scores")
   447→    if not isinstance(old_start, dict) or not old_start:
   448→        return
   449→    if scores.strict is None:
   450→        return
   451→
   452→    plan["previous_plan_start_scores"] = dict(old_start)
   453→    plan["plan_start_scores"] = {
   454→        "strict": scores.strict,
   455→        "overall": scores.overall,
   456→        "objective": scores.objective,
   457→        "verified": scores.verified,
   458→    }
   459→
   460→
   461→__all__ = [
   462→    "PendingImportScoresMeta",
   463→    "ScoreSnapshot",
   464→    "import_scores_meta_matches",
   465→    "pending_import_scores_meta",
   466→    "sync_communicate_score_needed",
   467→    "sync_create_plan_needed",
   468→    "sync_import_scores_needed",
   469→    "sync_score_checkpoint_needed",
   470→]
   471→
```

Good question. Let me trace the exact trigger-specific parameters through the actual gate code.

There are only two trigger-specific inputs across all gates:

**1. `scores_just_imported`** — used by `sync_communicate_score_needed` to bypass two checks:
```python
# line 427: don't re-inject if already communicated this cycle
if "previous_plan_start_scores" in plan and not scores_just_imported:
    return _EMPTY()
# line 429: don't inject if reviews incomplete
if not scores_just_imported and not _no_unscored(state, policy):
    return _EMPTY()
```

**2. `assessment_mode + import_file + import_payload`** — used only by `sync_import_scores_needed`. But this gate is **never called from the scan path** — it's import-only. So it doesn't belong in the shared pipeline at all.

That leaves one trigger-specific input: `scores_just_imported`. Can we eliminate it?

Check 1 bypass: "already communicated this cycle" sentinel. The import pre-step can clear `previous_plan_start_scores` directly — that's a legitimate plan mutation meaning "communication is needed again." Gate fires naturally.

Check 2 bypass: "reviews incomplete." After a trusted import, all dimensions should have real scores (trusted/attested imports are full assessments). So `_no_unscored` returns True naturally. The bypass was defensive.

So: import pre-step clears the sentinel. Pipeline gate fires on its own preconditions. No flag needed. **But** — if a trusted import ever leaves dimensions unscored (partial assessment), communicate-score would wait instead of firing immediately. That's a behavioral narrowing. In practice trusted imports are complete, but it needs a test to pin that assumption.

For `sync_import_scores_needed`: it moves to the import pre-step (it's already import-only today). Not in the pipeline.

So the pipeline is genuinely `reconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict)`. Three arguments. No flags, no context object. The callers are:

```
# scan
reset_if_force_rescan(plan)
reconcile_issues(plan, state)
result = reconcile_plan(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict)
sync_plan_start_scores(plan, state)
sync_postflight_completion(plan, state)

# import
import_result = sync_review_delta(plan, state)       # inject_triage=False
import_scores_result = sync_import_scores_needed(...) # import-specific gate
if trusted: plan.pop("previous_plan_start_scores", None)  # clear sentinel
result = reconcile_plan(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict)
```

Triggers are one-time because they're direct plan mutations, not flags. Clearing `previous_plan_start_scores` is idempotent — doing it twice is harmless. `sync_import_scores_needed` has its own `already_in_queue` guard. The pipeline gates all have `if X_ID in order: return _EMPTY()` guards, so a second pipeline run is a no-op.

The one thing that's NOT clean: `auto_cluster_issues` doesn't have an internal mid-cycle guard. The scan path currently passes `skip_auto_cluster=mid_cycle`. To make the pipeline truly parameterless, the mid-cycle check needs to move inside `auto_cluster_issues` (or the pipeline wraps it with `if not is_mid_cycle(plan)`). That's a small engine change — `is_mid_cycle` is already in `sync.context`.

No functionality lost, with one edge case to pin with a test (partial trusted imports and the unscored check). Want me to rewrite the doc?

1→# Centralize Postflight Pipeline
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→
     5→Create a single engine-level postflight pipeline for the shared "objective execution queue drained -> inject follow-up work" sequence, and have both scan reconciliation and review-import sync call it.
     6→
     7→"Real queue drained" means the **objective execution queue** is empty — not "any tracked work exists" and not "triage has completed for review findings." Postflight work is follow-up that triggers only after objective items are resolved, and it is phase-ordered:
     8→
     9→- postflight assessment work (`subjective::...` reruns and persisted assessment requests)
    10→- postflight workflow items
    11→- postflight triage
    12→- postflight review defects
    13→
    14→This design works with these constraints:
    15→
    16→- The pipeline owns triage, otherwise the import path can still lose `workflow::create-plan`.
    17→- `target_strict` is always passed explicitly from callers.
    18→- `plan_start_scores` and postflight-scan completion stay in the scan command, not in engine pipeline code.
    19→- Lifecycle unification on `queue_context(...).snapshot.phase` is an intentional behavior change and must be tested as such.
    20→
    21→## Known Behavioral Deltas
    22→
    23→This unification intentionally changes some behaviors. These must be tested explicitly.
    24→
    25→### Lifecycle phase resolution
    26→
    27→The current post-import path (`_sync_lifecycle_phase_after_import`) uses a hand-rolled resolution with explicit flags: `has_initial_reviews`, `has_objective_backlog`, `has_postflight_review`, `has_postflight_workflow`, `has_triage`, `has_deferred`. The unified pipeline replaces this with `queue_context(...).snapshot.phase`.
    28→
    29→Expected deltas to verify:
    30→
    31→- **Deferred/temporary skips:** The import path currently checks `has_deferred` (temporary skip entries in `plan.skipped`) to resolve SCAN phase. Confirm `queue_context` snapshot replicates this or document the acceptable change.
    32→- **Assessment requests in state:** The import path checks `has_postflight_review` which includes assessment requests in state (not just policy unscored/stale). The unified snapshot should resolve these into a dedicated postflight assessment phase rather than flattening them into review-defect execution.
    33→- **Conditional execution:** The import path only syncs lifecycle when mutations are dirty. The pipeline always runs lifecycle (Step 7). Guard inside the pipeline: only persist and set `lifecycle_phase_changed=True` when the phase actually differs from the current persisted value, to avoid spurious log entries.
    34→- **Mid-cycle unscored dimensions:** The old import path returns REVIEW unconditionally when `policy.unscored_ids` is non-empty. The snapshot requires `fresh_boundary AND initial_review_items`, so mid-cycle it stays in EXECUTE. This is the correct behavior — mid-cycle shouldn't regress to REVIEW just because a new dimension appeared.
    35→
    36→### Workflow gate ordering
    37→
    38→The current `sync_plan_after_import` runs workflow gates (communicate-score, import-scores, create-plan) **before** `_sync_review_delta`. The new flow reverses this: review delta runs first (step 3), then the pipeline runs workflow gates (step 6). This is safe — none of the workflow gate predicates depend on `queue_order` contents that review delta mutates (they check policy dimensions, `plan_start_scores`, `assessment_mode`) — but it is an intentional change, not an accidental side effect.
    39→
    40→### Auto-clustering gate asymmetry
    41→
    42→The current post-import path **always** runs auto-clustering when there's a data change (no mid-cycle guard). The pipeline uses `skip_auto_cluster`, and the import caller passes `skip_auto_cluster=not has_data_change`. This preserves the "only cluster when data changed" behavior but is worth noting as a formalization — previously it was implicit via the conditional call site.
    43→
    44→## Implementation Changes
    45→
    46→### 1. Make review-import delta triage-optional
    47→
    48→Update `desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py` so `sync_plan_after_review_import(...)` takes `inject_triage: bool = True`.
    49→
    50→Behavior:
    51→
    52→- Default stays `True` so direct engine tests and existing callers keep current semantics.
    53→- When `inject_triage=False`, the function still:
    54→  - prunes stale review IDs from plan containers
    55→  - appends new review IDs to `queue_order`
    56→  - returns `ReviewImportSyncResult`
    57→- But it does not call `sync_triage_needed(...)`, and returns:
    58→  - `triage_injected=False`
    59→  - `triage_injected_ids=[]`
    60→  - `triage_deferred=False`
    61→
    62→This is the prerequisite that lets the new pipeline preserve `create-plan before triage`.
    63→
    64→### 2. Add engine postflight pipeline
    65→
    66→Create `desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/pipeline.py` with two dataclasses and one public function.
    67→
    68→Public types:
    69→
    70→- `PostflightContext`
    71→  - `scores_just_imported: bool = False`
    72→  - `current_scores: ScoreSnapshot | None = None`
    73→  - `assessment_mode: str | None = None`
    74→  - `import_file: str | None = None`
    75→  - `import_payload: dict | None = None`
    76→- `PostflightResult`
    77→  - `subjective: QueueSyncResult | None = None`
    78→  - `auto_cluster_changes: int = 0`
    79→  - `communicate_score: QueueSyncResult | None = None`
    80→  - `import_scores: QueueSyncResult | None = None`
    81→  - `create_plan: QueueSyncResult | None = None`
    82→  - `triage: QueueSyncResult | None = None`
    83→  - `lifecycle_phase: str = ""`
    84→  - `lifecycle_phase_changed: bool = False`
    85→  - `dirty` property aggregates all changes
    86→  - `workflow_injected_ids` property: collects IDs from `communicate_score`, `import_scores`, and `create_plan` results that were injected, for structured logging by callers
    87→
    88→Public function:
    89→
    90→- `run_postflight_gates(plan, state, *, policy, target_strict, cycle_just_completed=False, context=None, skip_subjective=False, skip_auto_cluster=False) -> PostflightResult`
    91→
    92→Pipeline order:
    93→
    94→1. `sync_subjective_dimensions(...)` unless `skip_subjective`
    95→2. `auto_cluster_issues(...)` unless `skip_auto_cluster`
    96→3. `sync_communicate_score_needed(...)`
    97→4. `sync_import_scores_needed(...)` only if `context.assessment_mode` is set
    98→5. `sync_create_plan_needed(...)`
    99→6. `sync_triage_needed(...)`
   100→7. Resolve lifecycle via `coarse_phase_name(queue_context(state, plan=plan, target_strict=target_strict).snapshot.phase)` and persist with `set_lifecycle_phase(...)`
   101→
   102→Phase invariants the pipeline must preserve:
   103→
   104→- Triaged review findings remain `postflight_review` — they must not be merged into execute-phase candidates while objective work remains.
   105→- Assessment work is its own postflight phase:
   106→  - synthetic reruns (`subjective::...`) and persisted assessment requests can coexist there
   107→  - raw review findings must not share that active execution phase
   108→- Assessment requests remain postflight-only; they do not count as objective work and do not bypass assessment gating.
   109→- Only objective-queue mutations may reopen the postflight scan boundary (clear the scan-complete marker). Postflight review / workflow / triage mutations must not.
   110→- All queue surfaces (`next`, `plan queue`, breakdown/progress) must consume the same canonical execution snapshot.
   111→
   112→Important details:
   113→
   114→- The pipeline stays engine-only. No app-layer imports.
   115→- The pipeline writes its own log entries via `append_log_entry` for each gate that fires. Same action names and detail shapes as the current `_sync_*_and_log` wrappers: `sync_subjective`, `auto_cluster`, `sync_communicate_score`, `sync_import_scores`, `sync_create_plan`, `sync_triage`, `sync_lifecycle_phase`. This eliminates the need for caller-side log wrappers entirely — callers only write caller-specific log entries (scan: `seed_start_scores`/`clear_start_scores`/`complete_postflight_scan`; import: `review_import_sync`).
   116→- `queue_context(...)` should receive `target_strict=target_strict` explicitly so snapshot decisions match caller policy.
   117→- `_has_postflight_review_work()` is deleted rather than moved.
   118→- Step 4 (`sync_import_scores_needed`) is naturally a no-op when `context.assessment_mode` is None — scan callers never set this, so no conditional needed at the call site.
   119→- Step 7 (lifecycle) should only persist and set `lifecycle_phase_changed=True` when the resolved phase differs from the currently persisted value, to avoid spurious log entries when nothing changed.
   120→
   121→Export from `desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/__init__.py`:
   122→
   123→- `PostflightContext`
   124→- `PostflightResult`
   125→- `run_postflight_gates`
   126→
   127→### 3. Refactor scan reconciliation to call pipeline
   128→
   129→In `desloppify/app/commands/scan/plan_reconcile.py`:
   130→
   131→Delete the scan-local gate wrappers that are replaced by pipeline output handling:
   132→
   133→- `_sync_subjective_and_log`
   134→- `_sync_auto_clusters_and_log`
   135→- `_sync_communicate_score_and_log`
   136→- `_sync_create_plan_and_log`
   137→- `_sync_triage_and_log`
   138→- `_sync_lifecycle_phase_and_log`
   139→
   140→Delete dead helpers:
   141→
   142→- `_has_postflight_review_work`
   143→- `_has_postflight_workflow_items`
   144→- `_has_triage_items`
   145→
   146→Keep scan-specific helpers in place:
   147→
   148→- `_reset_cycle_for_force_rescan` (pre-pipeline cycle state clearing)
   149→- `_sync_post_scan_without_policy` / `reconcile_plan_after_scan` (pre-policy issue reconciliation: dead refs, superseded entries, stale skips, epic cluster membership)
   150→- `_is_mid_cycle_scan`
   151→- `_seed_plan_start_scores`
   152→- `_clear_plan_start_scores_if_queue_empty`
   153→- `_has_objective_cycle`
   154→- `_mark_postflight_scan_completed_if_ready`
   155→- `_sync_plan_start_scores_and_log`
   156→- `_sync_postflight_scan_completion_and_log`
   157→
   158→Add a scan-only display function:
   159→
   160→- `_display_pipeline_results(result: PostflightResult, plan: dict, *, mid_cycle: bool)`
   161→
   162→This is a flat sequence of `if result.X and result.X.changes: print(...)` blocks reproducing the current user-facing colorized messages. No logging — the pipeline handles that internally.
   163→
   164→Special cases to preserve:
   165→
   166→- If `mid_cycle` and auto-cluster was skipped, print the dim "mid-cycle scan — skipping cluster regeneration" message.
   167→- If `result.triage.deferred` and `epic_triage_meta["triage_recommended"]` is set, print the dim "triage recommended after current work" message.
   168→
   169→Rewrite `_sync_post_scan_with_policy(...)` to:
   170→
   171→- compute `mid_cycle = _is_mid_cycle_scan(plan, state) or force_rescan`
   172→- build `PostflightContext(current_scores=ScoreSnapshot(...))`
   173→- call `run_postflight_gates(...)` with:
   174→  - `policy=policy`
   175→  - `target_strict=target_strict`
   176→  - `cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed`
   177→  - `context=ctx`
   178→  - `skip_auto_cluster=mid_cycle`
   179→- display and log from the `PostflightResult`
   180→- then run scan-only plan-start/postflight-scan sync when `not force_rescan`
   181→
   182→Do not move plan-start-score logic into the pipeline.
   183→
   184→### 4. Refactor review import sync to call pipeline
   185→
   186→In `desloppify/app/commands/review/importing/plan_sync.py`:
   187→
   188→Delete:
   189→
   190→- `_has_postflight_review_work`
   191→- `_has_deferred_disposition_work`
   192→- `_sync_lifecycle_phase_after_import`
   193→- `_sync_subjective_queue_after_import`
   194→- `_append_workflow_log_entries`
   195→- `_PlanImportMutations` — replaced by passing `import_result` and `pipeline_result` directly
   196→- `_record_workflow_change` — no longer needed without `_PlanImportMutations`
   197→- `_merge_pipeline_into_mutations` — never created; the adapter pattern it would have served is eliminated
   198→
   199→Keep (with modifications noted below):
   200→
   201→- `PlanImportSyncRequest`
   202→- `_ImportSyncInputs`
   203→- `_build_import_sync_inputs`
   204→- `_sync_review_delta` — returns `ReviewImportSyncResult | None` directly (no mutations struct)
   205→- `_print_new_review_items`
   206→- `_print_stale_review_prunes`
   207→- `_print_review_import_footer`
   208→- `_print_review_import_sync`
   209→- `_print_workflow_injected_message`
   210→- `_append_review_import_sync_log`
   211→
   212→Change `_sync_review_delta(...)`:
   213→

   215→- Return `ReviewImportSyncResult | None` directly instead of writing to a mutations struct.
   216→
   217→Update `_print_review_import_footer(...)`:
   218→

   220→- Add a `triage_injected: bool` parameter sourced from the pipeline result, and use it instead of `result.triage_injected`.
   221→- Update `_print_review_import_sync(...)` to accept and forward this flag.
   222→
   223→Update `_append_review_import_sync_log(...)`:
   224→
   225→- Change signature to `(plan, diff, *, import_result, pipeline_result, covered_ids)`. No mutations struct — read directly from the two canonical result types:
   226→  - `workflow_injected_ids`: from `pipeline_result.workflow_injected_ids`
   227→  - `stale_sync_injected/pruned`: from `pipeline_result.subjective` (if not None)
   228→  - `auto_cluster_changes`: from `pipeline_result.auto_cluster_changes`
   229→  - `triage_injected/ids/deferred`: from `pipeline_result.triage` (if not None)
   230→  - `new_ids`, `added_to_queue`, `stale_pruned_from_queue`: from `import_result` (if not None)
   231→- Per-gate log entries (`sync_communicate_score`, `sync_import_scores`, `sync_create_plan`, `sync_triage`, etc.) are now written by the pipeline itself — delete `_append_workflow_log_entries`.
   232→
   233→Rewrite `sync_plan_after_import(...)` flow to:
   234→
   235→1. load plan and compute `policy`, `target_strict`, `current_scores`, `trusted_score_import`
   236→2. build `sync_inputs`
   237→3. `import_result = _sync_review_delta(...)` — gated on `sync_inputs.has_review_issue_delta` (existing gate preserved), returns result directly
   238→4. compute `has_data_change = sync_inputs.has_review_issue_delta or bool(sync_inputs.assessment_keys)`
   239→5. build `PostflightContext(...)` with score/import metadata
   240→6. call `pipeline_result = run_postflight_gates(...)` with:
   241→   - `policy=policy`
   242→   - `target_strict=target_strict`
   243→   - `context=ctx`
   244→   - `skip_subjective=not has_data_change`
   245→   - `skip_auto_cluster=not has_data_change`
   246→7. `dirty = (import_result is not None) or pipeline_result.dirty`
   247→8. save if `dirty`
   248→9. `_append_review_import_sync_log(plan, diff, import_result=import_result, pipeline_result=pipeline_result, covered_ids=...)`
   249→10. display: `_print_review_import_sync(state, import_result, ..., triage_injected=bool(pipeline_result.triage and pipeline_result.triage.injected))`; `_print_workflow_injected_message(pipeline_result.workflow_injected_ids)`
   250→
   251→The entire function is now: load → review delta → pipeline → save → log → display. Two result objects flow straight through. No intermediate struct, no copying between dataclasses.
   252→
   253→### 5. Update package surface and imports
   254→
   255→Update imports to use the new pipeline module and public exports from `engine._plan.sync`.
   256→
   257→Do not keep duplicate local wrappers that only forward to the pipeline.
   258→
   259→## Test Plan
   260→
   261→### New tests
   262→
   263→Add `desloppify/tests/plan/test_postflight_pipeline.py` covering:
   264→
   265→- pipeline runs gates in the required order
   266→- `create-plan` can inject before triage when review delta ran first with `inject_triage=False`
   267→- `skip_subjective=True` prevents subjective queue mutations
   268→- `skip_auto_cluster=True` prevents cluster regeneration
   269→- import-scores gate only runs when `assessment_mode` is provided (naturally no-op when context fields are None)
   270→- lifecycle is persisted from `queue_context(...).snapshot.phase`
   271→- lifecycle is NOT persisted (and `lifecycle_phase_changed=False`) when resolved phase matches current persisted value
   272→- unified lifecycle behavior:
   273→  - initial-review phase only appears at a fresh boundary
   274→  - mid-cycle unscored dimensions do not force lifecycle back to `review`
   275→  - deferred/temporary skips resolve correctly vs. old hand-rolled import logic (see Known Behavioral Deltas)
   276→  - assessment requests in state resolve to a dedicated postflight assessment phase vs. old `has_postflight_review` check
   277→- second pipeline run is a no-op in terms of additional mutations
   278→- pipeline does not seed/clear `plan_start_scores`
   279→- pipeline does not mark postflight scan complete
   280→- force-rescan clears cycle state, then pipeline runs on clean slate without errors
   281→- `workflow_injected_ids` property aggregates correctly from individual gate results
   282→- **phase isolation:** objective work remaining + triaged review findings present => execution queue contains only objective items, review findings stay in postflight_review
   283→- **postflight partitioning:** postflight assessment items and review defects do not share the same active execution phase
   284→- **scan boundary:** postflight review mutation does not bounce queue back to `workflow::run-scan` or clear the scan-complete marker
   285→
   286→### Existing tests to update
   287→
   288→Update direct scan/import tests to match the new ownership split:
   289→
   290→- scan tests that import moved helpers should target the pipeline or retained scan-specific helpers
   291→- review-import tests should stop assuming `sync_plan_after_review_import()` injects triage in the app flow
   292→- source-contract tests should assert the new shape:
   293→  - import path calls `_sync_review_delta(...)`
   294→  - import path calls `run_postflight_gates(...)`
   295→  - workflow gate orchestration no longer appears inline in `sync_plan_after_import(...)`
   296→- keep engine direct tests for `sync_plan_after_review_import()` unchanged where possible, since default `inject_triage=True` preserves old behavior
   297→
   298→### Verification commands
   299→
   300→Run:
   301→
   302→1. `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/ -q`
   303→2. `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/test_lifecycle_transitions.py -v`
   304→3. `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/engine/test_sync_split_modules_direct.py -v`
   305→4. `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/review/test_review_importing_support_direct.py -v`
   306→5. `python -m pytest desloppify/tests/commands/scan/ -v`
   307→6. `python -m desloppify --lang python scan --path desloppify`
   308→
   309→## Assumptions And Defaults
   310→
   311→- Lifecycle unification on snapshot phase is intentional and accepted as a behavior change.
   312→- `sync_plan_after_review_import(..., inject_triage=False)` is only for orchestrator use; engine direct callers keep default behavior.
   313→- The pipeline is postflight-only. Scan-specific plan-start-score and postflight-scan completion logic remain outside it.
   314→- Gate predicate semantics are preserved with three explicit exceptions:
   315→  - triage ownership moves to the pipeline
   316→  - lifecycle resolves from snapshot phase (see Known Behavioral Deltas)
   317→  - postflight assessment work and postflight review defects are separate active partitions, even though both still collapse to the coarse persisted `review` lifecycle
   318→- Queue mutation semantics around scan-boundary reopening are part of the contract — only objective-queue mutations may clear the scan-complete marker.
   319→- Incremental implementation order:
   320→  1. make review-import triage optional
   321→  2. add pipeline module and exports
   322→  3. refactor scan caller
   323→  4. refactor import caller
   324→  5. update/add tests
   325→

No context object. No skip parameters.\n\nEach caller follows the same shape:\n\n```\npre-step   →  reconcile  →  post-step  →  save  →  display\n(caller-specific)  (shared)  (caller-specific)\n```\n\n## Design Principles\n\n- **The pipeline doesn't know what triggered it.** Every gate's preconditions are derivable from plan and state. Trigger-specific context is expressed as direct plan mutations in the pre-step, not as parameters to the pipeline.\n- **Gates are self-gating.** Each gate checks its own preconditions and is a no-op when they aren't met. No caller-side skip flags.\n- **Triggers are one-time by construction.** Pre-step mutations are idempotent plan state changes (clearing sentinels, writing pending metadata). Snapshot requires `fresh_boundary AND initial_review_items`, so mid-cycle stays in EXECUTE. This is correct — mid-cycle shouldn't regress to REVIEW.\n- **Lifecycle only persists on change.** Import path only syncs lifecycle when mutations are dirty. Pipeline always runs lifecycle but only persists when the phase actually differs from the current value.\n\n### Workflow gate ordering\n\nCurrent import path runs workflow gates before review delta. New flow: review delta runs in the pre-step, then the pipeline runs workflow gates. Safe — no workflow gate predicates depend on `queue_order` contents that review delta mutates.\n\n### Communicate-score bypass narrowing\n\nCurrent import path passes `scores_just_imported=True` to bypass the `_no_unscored` check. New design: import pre-step clears `previous_plan_start_scores` sentinel, and the gate fires on its own preconditions. If a trusted import scores all dimensions (the normal case), `_no_unscored` returns True naturally. If a partial trusted import leaves dimensions unscored, communicate-score waits for remaining reviews — a behavioral narrowing. Pin with a test.\n\n## Implementation Changes\n\n### 1. Add internal mid-cycle guard to auto-clustering\n\nUpdate `auto_cluster_issues(...)` in `desloppify/engine/_plan/auto_cluster.py` to return 0 immediately when `is_mid_cycle(plan)` is True.\n\nCurrently the mid-cycle guard lives in the scan caller (`skip_auto_cluster=mid_cycle`). Moving it into the function makes auto-clustering self-gating, which eliminates the last skip flag from the pipeline.\n\n### 3. Add the reconciliation pipeline\n\nCreate `desloppify/engine/_plan/sync/pipeline.py`.\n\nPublic type:\n\n- `ReconcileResult`\n  - `subjective: QueueSyncResult | None = None`\n  - `auto_cluster_changes: int = 0`\n  - `communicate_score: QueueSyncResult | None = None`\n  - `create_plan: QueueSyncResult | None = None`\n  - `triage: QueueSyncResult | None = None`\n  - `lifecycle_phase: str = \"\"`\n  - `lifecycle_phase_changed: bool = False`\n  - `dirty` property: True if any gate changed anything\n  - `workflow_injected_ids` property: IDs from `communicate_score` and `create_plan` that were injected\n\nPublic function:\n\n```python\ndef reconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict) -> ReconcileResult\n```\n\nInternal pipeline order:\n\n1. Compute `policy = compute_subjective_visibility(state, target_strict, plan)`\n2. Compute `cycle_just_completed = not plan.get(\"plan_start_scores\")`\n3. Compute `current_scores` from `score_snapshot(state)`\n4. `sync_subjective_dimensions(plan, state, policy=policy, cycle_just_completed=cycle_just_completed)`\n5. `auto_cluster_issues(plan, state, target_strict=target_strict, policy=policy)` — self-gating, no-op mid-cycle\n6. `sync_communicate_score_needed(plan, state, policy=policy, current_scores=current_scores)` — no `scores_just_imported` param; sentinel clearing is the pre-step's job\n7. `sync_create_plan_needed(plan, state, policy=policy)`\n8. `sync_triage_needed(plan, state, policy=policy)`\n9. > AGENT

Done. The doc is rewritten around the core insight: the pipeline is `reconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict)`. Three arguments. No flags, no context object, no skip parameters.

Key changes from the previous version:

- **Killed `PostflightContext`** — trigger-specific context is expressed as direct plan mutations in the pre-step (clearing `previous_plan_start_scores`, calling `sync_import_scores_needed`), not as parameters.
- **Killed skip flags** — `auto_cluster_issues` gets an internal mid-cycle guard (step 2 in implementation order). All other gates were already self-gating.
- **Killed `scores_just_imported` parameter** — import pre-step clears the sentinel directly; gate fires on its own preconditions.
- **Moved `sync_import_scores_needed` out of the pipeline** — it's import-only (scan never calls it), so it belongs in the import pre-step.
- **Pipeline computes its own policy and scores** — `compute_subjective_visibility`, `score_snapshot`, `cycle_just_completed` are all derived internally.
- **Renamed to `reconcile_plan` / `ReconcileResult`** — it's not a "postflight pipeline," it's plan reconciliation.
- **Import caller is actual pseudocode** — setup, pre-step, reconcile, save+log+display. Linear, readable, no intermediate structs.

> DEVELOPER

so now it'll just be triggered at specific points either beginning or end of queue?

> AGENT

No — it runs after every mutation, not at specific queue boundaries. The two current call sites are:

1. **After scan** — new findings merged into state
2. **After review import** — review data arrived

The pipeline doesn't know or care where in the cycle it is. It just looks at current plan + state and does whatever is needed. Most gates are naturally no-ops mid-cycle:

- Auto-clustering: no-op (internal mid-cycle guard)
- Communicate-score: no-op (unscored dims remain, or already in queue)
- Create-plan: no-op (reviews incomplete, or already in queue)
- Triage: deferred (objective backlog exists)

The gates that DO fire mid-cycle are the ones that should — subjective dimension sync can inject newly-stale dimensions, lifecycle phase updates to reflect current state.

So the queue boundaries aren't triggers — they're emergent. When the objective queue drains, suddenly the gate preconditions are met: communicate-score fires (no unscored dims), create-plan fires (objective backlog exists), triage fires (review issues changed). The pipeline didn't "know" the queue drained. It just ran the same checks it always runs, and this time they passed.

That's why three arguments is enough. The pipeline is a pure function of reality.

> DEVELOPER

but it should show at tehe beginningn of the queue (nothing) or when the queue is cleared.

> AGENT

Right — and that's exactly what happens, but via two separate mechanisms:

**Injection** (the pipeline) and **visibility** (the queue snapshot) are independent.

The pipeline injects items into `queue_order` whenever preconditions are met. Communicate-score might get injected mid-cycle as soon as all reviews are done — even while objective work is still in progress. But it doesn't *show* yet.

The queue snapshot (`build_queue_snapshot` → `_phase_for_snapshot`) controls what's visible:

```
anchored_execution_items exist?  →  EXECUTE phase  →  show only objective items
queue drains, workflow items in queue?  →  WORKFLOW_POSTFLIGHT  →  show communicate-score
```

So at queue start: gates don't fire (unscored dims exist, no baseline to communicate). Nothing injected. Nothing shown.

Mid-cycle: communicate-score might inject (reviews done), but phase is EXECUTE, so it sits in `queue_order` invisibly. `desloppify next` shows the next objective item.

Queue clears: phase transitions to WORKFLOW_POSTFLIGHT. Communicate-score becomes visible. `desloppify next` shows it.

The pipeline doesn't need to know about visibility — it just makes the queue consistent. The snapshot decides what to surface and when. Same canonical snapshot, consumed by every surface (`next`, `plan queue`, `status`).

> DEVELOPER

So you have clear entry points that can only be triggered at those pints?

> AGENT

Yes. Exactly two entry points, each triggered by a data-ingestion event:

1. **After scan** — `reconcile_plan_post_scan()` in scan command
2. **After review import** — `sync_plan_after_import()` in import command

Nothing else triggers reconciliation. Display commands (`next`, `plan queue`, `status`, `show`) read the snapshot but never mutate. Action commands (`resolve`, `plan override`, `plan triage`) make targeted mutations to specific plan fields but don't run full reconciliation — they maintain their own invariants locally.

The two entry points are clean because they correspond to the two moments when external data enters the system: scan discovers the codebase state, import brings review judgments. Everything else is the user working through what's already queued.

> DEVELOPER

> DEVELOPER

WHEN WE START AND WHEN THE QUEUE HAS BEEN CLEARED

> AGENT

You're right. I was describing the mechanical trigger points, not the conceptual model.

The reconciliation has two meaningful moments:

1. **Queue starts** — fresh boundary, no `plan_start_scores` → inject initial subjective reviews, set up the cycle
2. **Queue clears** — objective work drained → inject postflight work (communicate-score, create-plan, triage)

That's it. Everything in between is just executing work items. The gates naturally express this — they check "am I at boundary 1?" or "am I at boundary 2?" and are no-ops otherwise.

Scans and imports are just the events that *can cause* a boundary transition. A scan might reveal objective work is resolved. An import might complete the last review. But the reconciliation doesn't care about scans or imports — it checks whether a boundary was reached.

Should I reframe the doc around these two entry points instead of "after scan / after import"?

> AGENT

Yes — and to your follow-up: exactly right. Within each phase, only the items belonging to that phase show. They run in the predetermined order. And they cannot show outside their phase.

The queue snapshot enforces all three:

1. **Only phase items show.** `_execution_items_for_phase` returns only the items for the current phase — objective items during EXECUTE, workflow items during WORKFLOW_POSTFLIGHT, triage during TRIAGE_POSTFLIGHT, etc.

2. **Predetermined order.** `normalize_queue_workflow_and_triage_prefix` enforces the prefix ordering in `queue_order`: workflow items first (communicate-score → import-scores → create-plan), then triage stages. The pipeline injects in this order and the normalization keeps it.

3. **Can't show outside their phase.** `_phase_for_snapshot` is a strict priority cascade — if `anchored_execution_items` exist, phase is EXECUTE, period. Workflow items exist in `queue_order` but are invisible until the phase transitions. No leaking.

Should I add these three invariants explicitly to the doc?

> DEVELOPER

tasks/centralize-postflight-pipeline.md still mostly makes sense,
  but it is incomplete.

  The bigger issue is not just postflight ordering. It is split ownership of “what is live execution work”
  across:

  - plan tracking in schema/init.py
  - phase resolution in snapshot.py
  - scan/import mutation paths that can leave queue_order, overrides, and clusters out of sync

  The reigh bug is a good example:

  - the plan still owned real objective work through overrides/clusters
  - queue_order no longer listed it
  - phase logic interpreted that as “objective work drained”
  - next fell into postflight even though action work still existed

  So centralize-postflight-pipeline.md is directionally right, but too narrow. Right now it assumes the main
  problem is “how postflight gates are ordered once execution drains.” What we just hit is one level
  earlier:

  - the system needs a single canonical definition of executable objective work
  - postflight must only begin after that canonical objective execution set is empty
  - callers should not infer “drained” from queue_order shape alone

  What I would change in the doc:

  - add an explicit invariant:
      - postflight gates may run only when canonical executable objective work is empty
      - canonical objective execution must recover plan-owned work from overrides/manual cluster state, not
        just queue_order
  - add a failure mode note:
      - queue_order can drift away from tracked plan ownership
      - lifecycle must not treat that drift as objective completion
  - expand the test plan with the exact regression:
      - tracked objective work exists only via overrides/manual clusters
      - queue_order contains only workflow/review/subjective items
      - snapshot must still resolve PHASE_EXECUTE

  So:

  - yes, this is a bigger architectural issue
  - yes, the centralization task still makes sense
  - but it should be reframed around canonical execution ownership, not only postflight gate unification

1→# Unify Plan Reconciliation
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→
     5→The plan queue has exactly two boundary transitions:
     6→
     7→1. **Queue starts** — fresh boundary, no active cycle → inject initial subjective reviews
     8→2. **Queue clears** — objective execution work drained → inject postflight work (communicate-score, create-plan, triage, stale dimension reruns)
     9→
    10→Reconciliation runs at these two boundaries and nowhere else. Everything between them is just executing work items — no gates fire, no workflow items inject, no queue composition changes.
    11→
    12→A single engine-level function handles both boundaries:
    13→
    14→```
    15→reconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict)
    16→```
    17→
    18→Three arguments. No flags. No context object. It detects which boundary (if any) was reached and acts accordingly. If neither boundary was reached, it's a no-op.
    19→
    20→Scan and import are just the events that can *cause* a boundary transition. After data ingestion (adding findings, syncing review deltas), the caller checks whether a boundary was crossed and calls `reconcile_plan` only if so.
    21→
    22→```
    23→data ingestion  →  boundary crossed?  →  reconcile  →  post-step  →  save  →  display
    24→(caller-specific)     (check)            (shared)     (caller-specific)
    25→```
    26→
    27→## Design Principles
    28→
    29→- **Two entry points, not two callers.** The entry points are "queue starts" and "queue clears" — not "after scan" and "after import." Scan and import are just events that can trigger a boundary check.
    30→- **The pipeline doesn't know what triggered it.** It derives which boundary was reached from plan + state. Trigger-specific context is expressed as direct plan mutations before the boundary check, not as parameters.
    31→- **Gates are self-gating.** Each gate checks its own preconditions. At queue-start, postflight gates don't fire (reviews incomplete). At queue-clear, initial-review gates don't fire (already reviewed). Mid-cycle, nothing fires.
    32→- **Triggers are one-time by construction.** Pre-step mutations are idempotent plan state changes (clearing sentinels, writing pending metadata). A second reconcile call is a no-op.
    33→- **The pipeline owns its own logging.** `append_log_entry` is engine-layer. Per-gate log entries are written inside the pipeline. Callers only write caller-specific logs.
    34→
    35→## Known Behavioral Deltas
    36→
    37→### Lifecycle phase resolution
    38→
    39→The current post-import path (`_sync_lifecycle_phase_after_import`) uses hand-rolled flags: `has_initial_reviews`, `has_objective_backlog`, `has_postflight_review`, `has_postflight_workflow`, `has_triage`, `has_deferred`. The pipeline replaces this with `queue_context(...).snapshot.phase`.
    40→
    41→Expected deltas to verify:
    42→
    43→- **Deferred/temporary skips:** Import path checks `has_deferred` → SCAN. Confirm snapshot replicates via `build_deferred_disposition_item`.
    44→- **Assessment requests:** Import path checks `has_postflight_review` including assessment requests in state. Confirm snapshot captures these in `postflight_review_items`.
    45→- **Mid-cycle unscored dimensions:** Import path returns REVIEW when `policy.unscored_ids` is non-empty. Snapshot requires `fresh_boundary AND initial_review_items`, so mid-cycle stays in EXECUTE. This is correct — mid-cycle shouldn't regress to REVIEW.
    46→- **Lifecycle only persists on change.** Import path only syncs lifecycle when mutations are dirty. Pipeline always runs lifecycle but only persists when the phase actually differs from the current value.
    47→
    48→### Workflow gate ordering
    49→
    50→Current import path runs workflow gates before review delta. New flow: review delta runs in the pre-step, then the pipeline runs workflow gates. Safe — no workflow gate predicates depend on `queue_order` contents that review delta mutates.
    51→
    52→### Communicate-score bypass narrowing
    53→
    54→Current import path passes `scores_just_imported=True` to bypass the `_no_unscored` check. New design: import pre-step clears `previous_plan_start_scores` sentinel, and the gate fires on its own preconditions. If a trusted import scores all dimensions (the normal case), `_no_unscored` returns True naturally. If a partial trusted import leaves dimensions unscored, communicate-score waits for remaining reviews — a behavioral narrowing. Pin with a test.
    55→
    56→## Implementation Changes
    57→
    58→### 1. Make review-import delta triage-optional
    59→
    60→Update `sync_plan_after_review_import(...)` in `desloppify/engine/_plan/reconcile_review_import.py` to take `inject_triage: bool = True`.
    61→
    62→- Default `True` preserves behavior for direct engine callers and tests.
    63→- When `False`: still prunes stale IDs, appends new IDs, returns `ReviewImportSyncResult` — but does not call `sync_triage_needed(...)`. Returns `triage_injected=False`, `triage_injected_ids=[]`, `triage_deferred=False`.
    64→
    65→This is the prerequisite that lets the pipeline run `create-plan` before `triage`.
    66→
    67→### 2. Add internal mid-cycle guard to auto-clustering
    68→
    69→Update `auto_cluster_issues(...)` in `desloppify/engine/_plan/auto_cluster.py` to return 0 immediately when `is_mid_cycle(plan)` is True.
    70→
    71→Currently the mid-cycle guard lives in the scan caller (`skip_auto_cluster=mid_cycle`). Moving it into the function makes auto-clustering self-gating, which eliminates the last skip flag from the pipeline.
    72→
    73→### 3. Add the reconciliation pipeline
    74→

    76→

   108→After each gate that fires, write a log entry via `append_log_entry`. Same action names and detail shapes as the current `_sync_*_and_log` wrappers.

  "new_string": "# Unify Plan Reconciliation\n\n## Summary\n\nThe plan queue has exactly two boundary transitions:\n\n1. **Queue starts** — fresh boundary, no active cycle → inject initial subjective reviews\n2. **Queue clears** — canonical executable objective work is empty → inject postflight work (communicate-score, create-plan, triage, stale dimension reruns)\n\nReconciliation runs at these two boundaries and nowhere else. Everything between them is just executing work items — no gates fire, no workflow items inject, no queue composition changes.\n\nA single engine-level function handles both boundaries:\n\n```\nreconcile_plan(plan, state, *, target_strict)\n```\n\nThree arguments. No flags. No context object. It detects which boundary (if any) was reached and acts accordingly. If neither boundary was reached, it's a no-op.\n\nScan and import are just the events that can *cause* a boundary transition. After data ingestion (adding findings, syncing review deltas), the caller checks whether a boundary was crossed and calls `reconcile_plan` only if so.\n\n```\ndata ingestion  →  boundary crossed?  →  reconcile  →  post-step  →  save  →  display\n(caller-specific)     (check)            (shared)     (caller-specific)\n```\n\n## Prerequisite: Canonical Executable Objective Work\n\nBefore this reconciliation can be correct, the system needs a single canonical definition of what constitutes executable objective work. This is the foundation — if boundary detection is wrong, the pipeline fires at the wrong time.\n\n### The problem\n\n\"Executable objective work\" is currently split across:\n\n- **`queue_order`** in `schema/__init__.py` — the ordered list of issue IDs\n- **`overrides`** and **`clusters`** in the plan — manual promotions and grouped work that track real objective items\n- **Phase resolution** in `snapshot.py` — `_phase_for_snapshot` which determines whether we're in EXECUTE\n\nThese can drift out of sync. The reigh bug is the canonical example:\n\n- The plan still owned real objective work through overrides/manual clusters\n- `queue_order` no longer listed those items\n- Phase logic interpreted that as \"objective work drained\"\n- `next` fell into postflight even though actionable work still existed\n\n### The invariant\n\n**Postflight gates may run only when canonical executable objective work is empty.** Canonical executable objective work must recover plan-owned work from overrides and manual cluster state, not just `queue_order`. If the plan tracks an objective item via any ownership mechanism (queue_order, overrides, manual clusters), that item is executable objective work.\n\n### The fix\n\n`_phase_for_snapshot` already computes `anchored_execution_items` from `_tracked_plan_ids(plan) | auto_promoted_ids`. This is the canonical set. The boundary check for reconciliation must use this same set — not `queue_order` shape alone, not a separate helper with different logic.\n\n### Failure mode to guard against\n\n`queue_order` can drift from tracked plan ownership (items removed from queue_order but still in overrides/clusters). Lifecycle must not treat that drift as objective completion. The boundary check and all queue surfaces must agree on one definition.\n\n## Design Principles\n\n- **Two entry points, not two callers.** The entry points are \"queue starts\" and \"queue clears\" — not \"after scan\" and \"after import.\" Scan and import are just events that can trigger a boundary check.\n- **The pipeline doesn't know what triggered it.** It derives which boundary was reached from plan + state. Trigger-specific context is expressed as direct plan mutations before the boundary check, not as parameters.\n- **Boundary detection uses canonical execution.** \"Queue clears\" means `anchored_execution_items` is empty — the same set that `_phase_for_snapshot` uses. Not `queue_order` length, not a separate predicate.\n- **Gates are self-gating.** Each gate checks its own preconditions. At queue-start, postflight gates don't fire (reviews incomplete). At queue-clear, initial-review gates don't fire (already reviewed). Mid-cycle, nothing fires.\n- **Items only show in their phase.** Phase determines execution visibility. Workflow items injected at queue-clear are invisible during EXECUTE. Review findings are invisible during EXECUTE. The snapshot enforces this — `_execution_items_for_phase` returns only items for the current phase. Items cannot leak across phase boundaries.\n- **Predetermined order within each phase.** Workflow items run in fixed order: communicate-score → import-scores → create-plan. Then triage stages. `normalize_queue_workflow_and_triage_prefix` enforces this in `queue_order`.\n- **Triggers are one-time by construction.** Pre-step mutations are idempotent plan state changes (clearing sentinels, writing pending metadata). A second reconcile call is a no-op.\n- **The pipeline owns its own logging.** `append_log_entry` is engine-layer. Per-gate log entries are written inside the pipeline. Callers only write caller-specific logs."

> AGENT

No, that feedback is right and it's an important architectural point. The doc was too narrow — it was about gate ordering when the real issue is one level earlier: **what counts as "objective work still exists."**

The reigh bug proves it. `queue_order` drifted from what the plan actually owned. The system thought work was drained when it wasn't. Gate ordering doesn't matter if the boundary detection is wrong.

The doc now has:
- A **Prerequisite** section that defines canonical executable objective work and the drift failure mode
- **Boundary detection uses canonical execution** as an explicit design principle
- **Phase isolation, predetermined order, no cross-phase leaking** as explicit principles
- The **reigh regression** and **queue-order drift** as specific test cases
- Step 0 in implementation order: fix canonical execution ownership before touching gates

> DEVELOPER

so is this now elegant?

> AGENT

Yes. Here's why.

The entire design reduces to one sentence: **reconciliation runs at queue boundaries and nowhere else, using one canonical definition of what work exists.**

Everything follows from that:

- Three-argument function — because the boundary is detectable from plan + state
- No flags — because gates check their own preconditions against that canonical set
- No context object — because trigger-specific context is a plan mutation, not a parameter
- Items can't leak across phases — because visibility is determined by the same canonical set
- The reigh bug can't happen — because "work drained" and "phase is EXECUTE" use the same definition

The pre-step / boundary-check / reconcile / post-step flow is the same for every caller. The callers differ only in what data they ingest and what they display. The reconciliation is identical.

The one thing I'd tighten before implementing: the doc is 300 lines because it accumulated detail across our iterations. The architecture is ~30 lines of actual design. The rest is operational detail (behavioral deltas, delete/keep lists, test cases). Before starting implementation I'd trim the operational sections to match the simplicity of the architecture — but the architecture itself is clean.
